# Delivery Racer: ideas checklist

Working list, from the owner's ideas of 2026-10-09. `[x]` done, `[~]` partly done (see the note),
`[ ]` not started, `[-]` built and then taken out. Each done item says what was and was not verified.

## State on 14-Oct (read with `src/delivery/HANDOVER-orchestrator-14-Oct.md`)

`main` is v1.1.129 (14-Oct, merged at the owner's "Merge into main" / "Push first": the ten idea cars of item 260 as items 263-267 (batches D1-D5, textures removed), nine more idea cars as items 270-274 (batches E1-E5; the Cinquecento was discarded), the global wheel colour 268, wheel designs 269, the Cosmetics equip screen 276 and the car catalogue 275; before that v1.1.128: true speed 257, the four damage stages v2 258 / 262, the handover docs). Waiting on the owner: 259 (cosmetics: wheels first, colour done as 268, designs next), 261 (level clocks after true speed). The rules for working here are in `CLOUD-AGENT-README.md`.

- [x] 86. Special events, merged in v1.1.9 (built on the feature branch `claude/special-events`; what was built and checked: `src/delivery/EVENTS-NOTES.md`). Checked before the merge, at the owner's word (rush it out): the tiers group with `.events-check`, the schema, tank and replay-store checks, `scripts/events.mjs check`; NOT the long groups (levels, replay determinism, smoke). Asked for (the owner, 11-Oct): within Events, a series of stages played in turn, each opening on its own date, all closing with the event; a required car (owned) or a free try (lent, as the special levels' cars are) per stage; a screen of stage cards with each stage's car in 3D at one pose (like Asphalt 9's car hunt); a limited car as the grand prize. Phases: rules and data, the screen, the prize car, a test event. Hold the merges.
- [ ] 77. Timed challenges (two players, or a daily challenge, 29) need FULLY SEEDED conditions (the owner, 11-Oct). Today the same two seeds (a replay's load and run seeds) give the same level and the same start, but not the same run: a mystery is rolled when it is picked up, from the one stream of dice the whole run shares (traffic, police and hazards draw from it too, as much as the driving makes them), and weighted by the player's social standing; so two players meet different mysteries. Needed: each mystery box (and other pickups' and hazards' chances, and if it is to be fully fair the traffic dealt out) drawing from dice of its own, from the seed and which box it is, so box 3 is the same for everyone whenever it is reached; no standing weighting in a challenge; a challenge code (level, side, car, both seeds) to share; then a friend's replay as the ghost. Changing how mysteries roll changes the rules (version.js logic): every replay and ghost recorded before it stops playing, so better done early.

## Technical

- [x] 52. Save data. Measured first (`scripts/.save-check.mjs`): a full save's cookie (40 levels on both sides, 33 cars) was 4013 bytes of the 4096 allowed, so three more levels would have lost it. Now local storage is read first and the cookie only if it has nothing; best times are saved to 0.1 s and the bank to the cent, which brings the same save's cookie to 2931 bytes; and the menu has Export save and Import save (`render/savecode.js`), a code of about 2,600 characters that is checked and tidied on the way in. Checked headless: sizes, a code out and back, five bad codes refused. Both panels seen in a screenshot (`?savepanel`, `?savepanel=import`); the Copy and Load buttons were not clicked, and the menu's refresh after an import was not seen.

- [ ] 115. IDEA ONLY, nothing built (the owner, 12-Oct): ways of getting cars. (a) Buy in the garage (as now). (b) Win from a level or special event (as now: `reward`, `prize`). (c) Blueprints: each car starts at 0/X blueprints and locked; X blueprints unlocks it, at ECU0; ECU0 cars are uncompetitive for their tier; each further X blueprints raises it one ECU; each ECU raises its stats; at max ECU it outperforms any garage car. Event cars get a magenta star tier (undecided whether that makes them wildcards). Open: where blueprints come from, what X is per car, how many ECUs, whether bought cars can be upgraded too, how tier rules (car locks, `allowedCars`, tier-money) treat a car whose stats change, and replays/ghosts (a car's fingerprint covers its figures, so an ECU change invalidates old replays and ghosts of it).
  - Item 115, owner's answers (12-Oct): blueprints come from a totally separate economy from the game's (money, tips); which one is undecided. The number of ECU levels varies car to car. Garage cars might have ECUs too: upgraded less, and even at their top still weaker than, or middling against, the rest of their tier (the event cars are the ones that overtake garage cars at max ECU).
  - Item 115, replays (owner, 12-Oct): the answer to ECU changes breaking replays is to record the car's stats as they were in the replay itself (so an ECU upgrade, or a rebalance, no longer invalidates it). To work out when built: record the figures the run used in the header (not only `carPrint`), play back with those; keep the car's id, model and perks checks; a ghost needs only the path, so it would not care.
  - Item 115, replays: DECIDE LATER (owner, 12-Oct). Recording only the player's car stats is not enough: everything in the run is driven by figures (traffic kinds, police, rivals, cargo, powerups, gimmicks), so a replay would have to keep the stats of everything in the level. And `cars.js` and the traffic tables are in the replay's `logic` hash (vite.config.js: every `src/delivery/*.js` not on NOT_RULES, with its imports), so any edit to them already refuses every replay, whatever the per-car fingerprint says (read, not tested). Two ways: (1) take the stat tables out of the hash, and have each recording carry a snapshot of every table it used, played back from the snapshot (old replays survive rebalances too; bigger header, bigger change); (2) keep the tables in the hash and record only a small per-car ECU modifier in the header (fixes upgrades, not rebalances; far smaller). Ghosts are unaffected either way (they read only the stored path).
  - Item 115, unlock kinds (owner, 12-Oct; this replaces the earlier "unlocked at ECU0"): ECU0 now means not driveable / locked. Two kinds of car. (1) KEY cars start at ECU1 and blueprints upgrade them, but they are not selectable (not unlocked) until their key is obtained; garage cars are key cars (the key is what they are bought or won with today). (2) BLUEPRINT cars start at ECU0 (locked) and are unlocked on reaching ECU1, so their first X blueprints are the unlock and the car then plays at ECU1. Open: what a key is for a won car (the reward/prize), whether blueprints collected for a key car before its key count, and what "uncompetitive for their tier" now means (it would be ECU1 of a blueprint car).
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): decided: a player CAN raise the ECU of a locked key car (blueprints count before the key is obtained); the car stays unselectable until the key, and then plays at whatever ECU it has reached.
  - Item 115, terminology (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only, nothing renamed in the game yet: the SHOWROOM is the new UI-based menu where cars are selected. The current garage stays as the place to buy the current cars, rebranded the CAR LOT, "Crazey Jim's Used Cars" (the owner's spelling). Tooltips on a car that cannot be driven yet: a key car (key not obtained): "You need the key to drive this car"; a garage car (not bought): "Purchase this car from the car lot". Note: "garage" is in the code, the menus, words, URLs (`?garage`, `garageTabBtn`...), CHECKLIST and the notes; a rename of the words players see is separate from renaming the code.
  - Item 115, card packs (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only: PACKS are the main source of blueprints, then special events. Some Career (main mode) levels offer a pack on first completion too. A pack holds cards of different RARITIES; a card is a blueprint for a car. COMMON (the bulk of a pack): blueprints for 1 to 4 star garage cars. UNCOMMON (in every pack): 5 star garage cars and all 1 to 4 blue star cars. EPIC: at least one card in every 10-pack is Epic or better; in a standard pack the Epics are all the 5 blue star cars plus a select few blueprint-only cars (the "evergreen" blueprint cars). LEGENDARY (above Epic): blueprint cars only. BESPOKE (the highest): the keys themselves are Bespoke cards. Open: pack sizes and kinds besides the standard and the 10-pack, odds per rarity, duplicates (a blueprint card for a car already at max ECU), what "blue star" and "magenta star" tiers each hold (tiers.json has stars today; the event cars were given magenta), how a Career first-completion pack differs from a bought one, and the separate economy that buys packs (undecided).
  - Item 115, pack kinds (owner, 12-Oct), ideas only: CAREER packs (first completion of some Career levels) contain only "evergreen" cars, which are the pack filler. SPECIAL packs have FEATURED cards (usually event cars): in each special pack the Epic-or-better slot is guaranteed to be a featured card, and every other slot has a chance to be upgraded; the featured cards drop at a 20% chance. Open (to read the 20% one way): is the 20% the chance, per other slot, that it upgrades to a featured card; or the chance that any given featured card comes up when a slot upgrades? And is the guaranteed featured slot still the pack's Epic-or-better one, with the 10-pack's one-Epic rule met by it?
  - Item 115, featured cards (owner, 12-Oct): the 20% is PER FEATURED CARD, rolled when a slot upgrades (so with several featured cards each has its own 20%). The LAST slot of a special pack is guaranteed a featured card, and it is also the pack's guaranteed Epic-or-better card (one slot doing both). Open: what a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card becomes (a higher rarity, a different card), and whether several featured cards can come up at once from one slot.
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): every special pack has at least one featured card: a special pack is just a career pack (evergreen filler) with the featured-cards mechanic added. Each slot holds exactly one card, so one slot can never give two featured cards. (Read, not stated: a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card stays its ordinary filler card. Say if not.)
  - Item 115 (owner, 12-Oct): a slot that upgrades but rolls no featured card upgrades to a higher-rarity FILLER (evergreen) card instead (this replaces my "stays ordinary" reading above).
  - Item 115, filler cars (owner, 12-Oct: promote some Idea cars to the main roster as filler cars, with a good mix of tiers). PROPOSED list, not coded, picked by the placeholder speed/health against the garage cars of each tier, and for a distinct idea (none repeats a garage car; the duplicate pairs still undecided are left out). Tier 1: Bubble Car, Milk Float, Tuk-Tuk. Tier 2: Woody Wagon, Panda Coupe, Kei Roadster. Tier 3: Fire Engine, Field Scout, Monster Truck. Tier 4: Stately Saloon, Teardrop Streamliner, Pony Car. Tier 5: Midnight Coupe, Split-Window Coupe, Wedge Supercar. Without the blueprint system they would have no way to be obtained, so they would be in the roster locked.
  - Item 115, balance (owner, 12-Oct), plan only: with filler cars and ECUs the power curve of the garage cars has to be flattened, and the strict rule that a lower-tier car is worse than a higher-tier one is dropped. Where it is enforced today: `scripts/.balance-check.mjs` (in the `.tiers-check` / levelrules runner group) holds each tier's Blue Star cars strictly below the best gold car of the tier above (or `NEXT_TIER_CAPS` at the top); the tier cap tables in cars.js feed it. Not changed yet. To decide when built: what replaces it (nothing; a looser band; per-tier ranges that may overlap), and the levels' clocks, which are timed to a tier's cars (item 80).
  - Item 115, balance (owner, 12-Oct): decided: there will be NO such rule any more (no replacement band between tiers); when built, `scripts/.balance-check.mjs`'s tier ceilings are removed, not rewritten. Still to do then: the level clocks (item 80).
  - Item 115, stats (owner, 12-Oct), plan only: RESPAWN TIME becomes a car statistic, and a higher-ECU car always respawns faster. To decide when built: whether the shield varies too, what the range is (the penalty is a cost to the clock, so it feeds the level clocks, item 80), garage cars' figures, races (the same stat or the race rules' own), and that it goes into the stats shown in the garage / showroom.

ECUs, blueprints, packs and the new stats (respawn time...) do not apply to them; races keep their own rules (`CONFIG.race`, including its own respawn time and shield). Open: which car a race uses (its own fixed figures, or a car's base ECU1 figures), to settle when built.
  - Item 115, balance (owner, 12-Oct): decided: the garage cars' CURRENT figures are kept as their MAXED ECU. Lower ECUs are then weaker than today (the cars start below their current power and climb to it), so the flattening of the curve is done by lowering the low ECUs, not by touching the maxed ones; every existing level clock and cap stays right for a maxed car. To decide: how far below the maxed figures ECU1 sits per car, the ECU count of each, and what a player with no blueprints yet drives (ECU1 garage cars are weaker than anything they drive now).

This leaves the event cars (Silver Comet etc., given a magenta star tier of their own) on the ladder, as before: their blueprints and max-ECU strength stand. Starter cars come maxed.

Gold (normal) stars: 1 star: ECU1, no cards (never upgraded; the starter cars, maxed already); 2 star: ECU2, common; 3 star: ECU3, common; 4 star: ECU4, common; 5 star: ECU5, uncommon. Blue stars: 1 blue: ECU3, uncommon; 2 blue: ECU4, uncommon; 3 blue: ECU5, uncommon; 4 blue: ECU5, epic; 5 blue: ECU5, epic. So the ECU count follows the tier (1 to 5), and a car's maxed figures are today's. Open: the size of a step for each kind (a share of the climb per ECU, per stat), and respawn time, which always improves with ECU on both kinds.
  - Item 115, respawn time (owner, 12-Oct), plan only, `CONFIG.respawnTime` NOT changed yet (still 4.5 s for every car): base respawn time 5.5 s at ECU1, and 0.5 s less for each ECU above 1: ECU1 5.5, ECU2 5.0, ECU3 4.5, ECU4 4.0, ECU5 3.5 s. By the ladder above that makes a 1-star car (ECU1 only) 5.5 s, a 5-star 3.5 s, a 3-blue-star or better 3.5 s. Special cars (ECU1 cap) 5.5 s; starter cars (maxed, 1-star) 5.5 s. Fixed by adding `.shop-screen, #showroom, #packs` to it (v1.1.21). BUILT: (a) ecus.json: every ECU of every car now also has `agility` (cars.js `agility`, steering), `delivery` (package efficacy, new), `cool` (the shoulder meter's fuse, new; the name COOL: 'cool' is otherwise only gunfire.js's private cooldown map, no stat or word), `crossing` (cars.js `crossing`: rails, fords, mud, the tide) and `weight` (cars.js `mass`); the first four by the same equal steps as the others (f 0.03 a car lot car, 0.06 a blueprint/event/Bespoke car; to 0.001 so each ECU is above the last), weight the same at every ECU; the maxed ECU = the car's own figure (agility, crossing, mass as today; cool 1.0 for every car). Written by `node scripts/ecu-tables.mjs --stats` (adds the fields a car's ECUs lack, by the file's own ECU count, keeping every hand edit; idempotent), `--missing` (new cars, with every field), `--delivery` (rewrites only `delivery`, from the table); `ecu.js` `NEW_STATS` says what each is at the max, `Ecu.stats(car, ecu)` returns them (an entry written before them: the car's maxed figures). (b) DELIVERY: each car's own figure at its max ECU, cooked up, in `ecu.js` `DELIVERY` (edit there, or a car's `delivery` field in cars.js, then `--delivery`), 0.6 to 1.6 with a reason per group: mail/courier vehicles best (Post Van and Courier Special 1.6, Milk Float 1.55, Kei Truck and Float Van 1.5, Road 8x8 1.45), vans 1.3-1.35, trucks and working machines 1.1-1.3, estates/SUVs 1.0-1.2, small cars 0.9-1.0, style cars 0.8-0.85, sports cars and the Junker 0.65-0.75, novelties, record cars and racers 0.6. What it scales (packages.js `deliver`): an Evil flaming package's damage (and so its spin-out and the driver's anger) and a Good gift's standing gain (`Social.gift(cop, efficacy)`); not a gift's 4-point scratch, moods, busts, Big Splash's reach or pelting. Never negative. (c) COOL: `Social.dangerTime` = CONFIG.dangerTime x max(CONFIG.coolFloor 0.6, the car's cool) x (1 + standing); 1.0 at every car's max (3 s, as today), e.g. 2.28 s at ECU1 of a 5-ECU blueprint car, 2.64 s at ECU1 of a 5-ECU car lot car. (d) The player's car takes all five at its ECU (`Player.agility`, `crossing`, `mass`, `delivery`, `cool`, all through `carStats`): every read of the player's agility, crossing and mass was through those getters (cornering, steering, rails, fords, mud, tide, collisions, ice weight); traffic's own figures untouched. Races, a level's own car, Car Swap and a Super car keep their own / maxed figures, as the other stats. (e) The showroom's detail: TOP SPEED, ACCELERATION, HEALTH, AGILITY, DELIVERY, COOL, CROSSING, WEIGHT, RESPAWN, the four that scale with the maxed ghost bar and "max n", Weight a plain bar; the five ratio stats SHOWN as whole numbers 0-100: shown = round(ratio x `STAT_SCALE` 50) held to 0..100 (`showroomData.js`; 1.0 = 50), the bar the same share; the game keeps the ratios; a tooltip on each new name. - [x] 137. The showroom landing looks SPRUNG (the owner, 12-Oct: "get the bounce to actually dampen the wheels on every car... it looks like there's suspension. Every car looks stiff right now"). Branch `worktree-agent-a2c3088c722b5d7e5`, not merged, no version bump. BUILT: `render/carsuspension.js` (`prepareSuspension(model)`, worked out once per model and kept on `userData.suspension`, and `applySuspension(model, body, o)`, used by `render/showroomview.js`, so it covers both the desktop full view and the phone's single-car screen). It finds each model's tyres by SHAPE however the model was built: a disc on its side that touches the floor (the shared `wheel()` helpers, a model's own CylinderGeometry, a torus), plus what sits inside its round (hub, spokes, whitewall, tread), and a flat decal on the floor (an underglow). Those are held on the floor while the body sinks onto them and rises off them, with a touch of pitch and roll, so the arches close and open round the tyres. The wheels fall with the car until it lands (`carbounce.js` now gives `wheelY`). A long car pitches less. A low body is eased off short of the floor (a soft bump stop: the Toybota and Float Van compress ~1.5-1.8 cm instead of 3.4). Any other part of a wheeled car that stands on the floor (the camper's awning poles) is stretched upright. The amplitude, timing (0.8 s), reduced motion (no motion) and silhouettes (fade-in only) are unchanged. NO BOUNCE (the owner: some vehicles need none): a vehicle with no wheels on the floor, or a model with `userData.noBounce`, only fades in: the Monowheel (`noBounce`: its carriage hangs from rollers on the hoop), the Saucer, the Jetboat, and the hovercraft and snowcat ideas. COVERAGE: 116 cars (the showroom's roster, which includes the 18 fillers, the 6 Bespoke key cars, the event and earned cars, plus every car idea). 110 have their wheels found by shape with no model touched, 1 also has stretched poles (the camper), and 5 do not bounce. `render/showcar.js` (`makeShowCar`, `paintShowCar`, moved out of garage.js, which re-exports them) lets a check build the models without the car lot. `?showroom&look=<id>&full&bounce=0.16` holds the landing at 0.16 s (the compression peak; 0.31 is the rebound) without the fade or the turning, for a picture. CHECKED: `scripts/.suspension-check.mjs` (in the render group) builds all 116 and, at the compression and rebound peaks and in the fall, checks that the wheels' lowest point does not move (planted), the body's middle moves by the offset and never into the floor, a no-bounce car does not move at all, and everything is back as before at rest: all ok. The words, render, tiers and cars groups passed (`--no-lock`; render and tiers again after the no-bounce change). The vite build passed, and node --check and eslint are clean on the changed files (ideaModels3.js has 3 eslint errors that were already there). SEEN in headless Chromium (swiftshader), held still, rest against compression: the Sportscompact, the Hot Hatch, the Love Bus, the 6x6, the Tuk-tuk (also the rebound), the Moon Rover, the Swan Car (Bespoke), and the Monowheel (no motion). In each, the body sat visibly lower over wheels that had not moved, with nothing detached or gaping. NOT SEEN: the landing in motion at real speed (only held frames were looked at, in a software renderer), a phone, and the idea cars in a picture (they are not on the showroom floor). Whether 3.4 cm of travel reads well enough at a glance is the owner's call.

ADDING A CAR MUST NOT INVALIDATE EVERY REPLAY (the owner, 12-Oct: "invalidating all replays because I added something is really harsh"): `ecus.json` is in the replay `logic` hash (vite.config.js: a file is in the hash unless it is on NOT_RULES), so adding one car's ECU table (item 166 added the Goddess Saloon's and the Fifties Hardtop's) changes the hash and every saved replay is refused. TO DO: stop hashing the whole file. Options, simplest first: (a) a replay records the ECU table entry it ran (its car's stats at its ECU) in its header and plays back from THAT, so the file need not be hashed at all (the replay already records the ECU level); (b) hash only each car's own entry and check the replay's car's entry, so adding or editing another car never refuses it; (c) at least leave added entries out of the hash and hash only the entries that existed. Also check which other data a new car touches that is hashed (cars.js stats are; a new car entry should not change an existing car's replays), and add a check: add a car to the tables and an old replay still plays. Not done; the Goddess/Hardtop promotion (166) refuses old replays until this is fixed.

We can budget more stats per ECU step, especially for Blueprint and Key cars. Consider upping the Composure at max of the lower tier cars (1 and 2 stars) as they take longer to overtake"; then "the stat difference between an ECU1 and ECU5 is too small"; then "Keep the 25% limit for Top Speed only"). Branch `claude/ecu-budget`, NOT merged, no version bump. AUDIT (before): one f for every stat (0.06 blueprint/event/Bespoke, 0.03 car lot), so a 5-ECU blueprint car's ECU1 was 76% of its max in EVERY stat, including top speed (max +32% over ECU1, over the owner's 25% limit), and the felt stats barely moved: a composure step 0.18 s (0.09 s for a lot car) of a 3 s meter, a crossing step about 1% of top speed kept on rails, a gift's standing +0.1 to 0.25 points a step, a 2-ECU lot car's single step 3% everywhere (0.8 m/s, 2 health). BUILT: (a) per-stat steps, ecu.js `STEPS` (blueprint/key: top speed 0.045, agility 0.08, accel/health/delivery/composure/crossing 0.10; car lot: 0.03, 0.04, 0.05), `TOP_SPEED_CAP` 1.25 (ECU1 >= max / 1.25) and `FLOORS` (ECU1 health >= 80, accel >= 7, agility >= 0.5, composure >= CONFIG.composureFloor 0.6), ECU1 by the step held to the floor then equal steps to the max; the maxed figures unchanged. A 5-ECU blueprint/key car's ECU1 is now 82% of its max top speed (was 76%: a fresh one is a little FASTER than before), 68% agility, 60% accel/health/delivery/composure/crossing (was 76%); a 5-ECU lot car's 88% / 84% / 80% (was 88% everywhere). E.g. the Midnight Coupe ECU1 37.7 m/s, 10.8 accel, 108 health, 1.8 s shoulder (was 35 / 13.7 / 137 / 2.28 s), ECU5 46 / 18 / 180 / 3 s. Floors that bind: the Solar Racer's and Monowheel's health (ECU1 80, was 68 and 76), the Sportscompact's and Kei Roadster's health and the Love Bus's accel (flat: their max is the floor). - [x] 183. Back from the Shop and the showroom goes to the menu cleanly (the owner, 13-Oct: on a phone, back did not return cleanly). What was wrong: `nav.js` read back by the depth number in `history.state` alone, and that state outlives the page. After a reload, or a phone that dropped the tab and loaded it again, with a screen open, the old entries were still there with their numbers: the next screen's entry came after them, so back landed on an old entry of the same or a higher number and closed nothing (the Shop or the showroom stayed up), and back from the menu took one or two dead presses before leaving the site. A forward step onto the entry of a screen closed by its own Back did nothing either. Also, the Shop opened from the showroom set `Game.inMenu` false on closing while the showroom was still up (Enter could start a run from it). Fixed: every entry carries its depth AND this load's mark; the menu's entry is taken over when the page loads; back closes the screens above the depth landed on; an earlier load's entries are stepped over, a forward step onto a closed screen is stepped back (`nav.js`); the Shop gives back the `Game.inMenu` it found (`render/shop.js`). New check `scripts/.nav-check.mjs` (a stand-in history: nested screens, own Back buttons, two closed at once, forward steps, reloads, a second round; 24 of its lines fail on the old `nav.js`), in the tiers group. Checked in headless Chromium at 390x844, touch, mobile, before (main) and after, each sequence twice in a row then one more back: menu > Shop > Back / back; menu > Showroom > Back / back; Showroom > a car's full view > Back > Back / back x2; Showroom > its Shop > Back > Back / back x2; full view > Shop > Back x3 / back x3; Showroom > Crazey Jim's > Back > Back / back x2; the filters sheet > Done > Back / back x2; a reload on the full view then the Shop and back; a reload on the Shop then the showroom and back. Before: 14 of 16 (both reload cases left the Shop or the showroom up); after: 16 of 16, the menu each time, the extra back leaving the site, no console errors; the menu's stills read. Also `.showroom-check`, `.shop-check`, `.packs-check`, the build. - [x] 212. WHAT'S INSIDE ON A HIGH-DPR TABLET (the owner's Xiaomi Pad 7: Android Chrome, desktop site, touch, device pixel ratio about 3, 144 Hz; flicker while scrolling and while still, cards flashing, poor performance). Branch `claude/mipad-inside`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 212 is taken). FOUND (headless Chromium through the DevTools protocol, desktop UA, touch emulation, which reports `pointer: coarse` like the tablet; 1280x850 and 1067x712 CSS px at ratio 1 and 3): NOT the WebGL side. No card has a WebGL context (one shared renderer), no context was lost, each still is capped at ratio 2 (367x275 px), 61 encoded for the standard pack's 66 cards, and every card ends as a background picture with no live canvas. The cause is a COMPOSITING-LAYER EXPLOSION: the shine's `mix-blend-mode: screen` (Epic, Legendary and Bespoke cards' `.bp-art::before`) blends with whatever lies behind the art, so Chrome cannot merge those cards into the scroller's layer. It gives them, and the cards painted after them, layers of their own. Standard pack: 136 composited layers (64 of them "Overlap"), 209 Mpx of layer area at ratio 3 (17 Mpx at ratio 1), 68 MB of GPU tiles once scrolled. Car Hunt pack at 1067x712: 105 layers. FIX: `isolation: isolate` on `.bp-art` (blueprintcard.css), so the blend stays inside the art, which is opaque: the same pixels. Standard: 136 to 85 layers, tiles 68 to 41 MB scrolled; Car Hunt: 105 to 32 layers, tiles 62 to 34 MB. Look: screenshots before and after at ratios 1, 2 and 3, scrolled to three places with animations frozen, differ only where a car's lazily drawn picture had or had not arrived yet (and the menu scene behind); the cards' styling is the same. Checks: .touch-check, .nav-check, .extras-hit-check, .packs-check and .shop-check pass (0 FAIL), vite build passes; eslint has nothing to check (a CSS change only), and its 13 errors in src/delivery were already on main. NOT verified: on a real Mi Pad 7 or any Android device (the tile-memory budget and GPU there cannot be emulated, and headless frame times under SwiftShader mean nothing), so whether this ends the flicker is unproven. Still standing: the Bespoke and Legendary-key cards' compositor animations (bp-glow-rainbow and bp-glow-gold animate `filter` on the whole card, bp-twinkle animates opacity; 21 layers and their overlaps), and the menu's 3D scene, which keeps drawing under the sheet (about 60 WebGL draw calls a frame) together with the menu's stage-drift layer (1792x1190 CSS px). These are the next suspects if the tablet still flickers. RESULT ON THE REAL TABLET (the owner, v1.1.98): "even worse if anything"; the `isolation: isolate` line is REVERTED in item 213 (headless layer counts proved nothing about the device).

Names: `glow` (the Legendary-key / Bespoke cards' bp-glow-gold / bp-glow-rainbow: `filter: drop-shadow` animated on the whole card), `twinkle` (bp-twinkle: opacity animated on .bp-art::after), `shine` (every .bp-art::before sweep / holo with its mix-blend-mode, and the Legendary blueprint's bp-shine), `blend` (only the shine's mix-blend-mode: the shine still moves), `drift` (the menu stage picture's stage-drift), `menu` (every menu animation: drift, the backdrop's light sweep, the START pulse, the LIVE dot), `scene` (while a sheet is up nothing else on the page is drawn: menu, Shop, game canvas, HUD hidden), `blur` (every backdrop-filter), `shadow` (cards' box-shadows, the pictures' drop-shadow filters, the stars' glow), `pics` (the car and key pictures on the cards hidden: image decode / GPU memory), `stacks` (the x1/x2 chance fades, the bubble's cycle, the sheet's 1.6 s timer), `anim` (every animation and transition in the sheet and the reveal), `all`. HOW THE OWNER USES IT: open `delivery.pingmathehippo.com/delivery/?lite=all` (once deployed), What's inside: if the flicker is gone, halve the list (`?lite=glow,twinkle,shine,blend,pics,shadow` then the other half) until one name is left. (3) ALWAYS ON (not a switch): while What's inside or the pack reveal is up, <html> has `sheet-up` (lite.js sheetUp, asked every frame by the menu stage's loop), the menu stage's 3D car and cargo are not drawn under it (headless 1280x850: about 1000 to 1500 WebGL draw calls a second under the sheet before, 0 now; drawn again once it closes) and the menu's and the Shop's animations under it are PAUSED (menu-sweep, stage-drift, start-pulse: they carry on where they were). - [x] 213. The car on the BIG pack of a pack opening was cut off on a larger screen (the owner, 13-Oct: "The pack art for the Silver Comet is cut off ... on a larger screen ... it's the big pack"). Branch `claude/comet-bigpack-fix`, NOT merged, no version bump. What was wrong, three things in how a pack's car still (render/packart.js packCar, carstill.js, modelviews.js drawStill) was drawn and shown: (1) `.shop-pack .pack-car` gave its background NO size, so the picture showed at its pixel size, and a still is drawn at the device pixel ratio: on a 2x screen (Retina, an iPad, most large high-DPI screens) a picture twice the box, only its top left quarter (the nose) seen; (2) the still was sized and keyed by `getBoundingClientRect`, the box ON SCREEN, which the big pack's shake (packs.css pr-shake: scale .6 to 1.7, turned) makes another size and shape than the span: drawn late in the shake (scaled up) it was bigger than the box, cut off even at 1x, and framed for the wrong shape; (3) it was queued AFTER the reveal's ten card stills (packs.js queues them first, the pack's car came a frame later), so on a large screen (five cards a row, bigger stills, more to draw) it was drawn late in the shake or not at all before the pack burst (in headless Chromium at 1280 wide and up, main's pack had no car at all during the shake). Fixed: modelviews.js `boxOf(el)` (the element's own laid-out size, offsetWidth/Height, no transforms) sizes drawStill and carstill.js keyOf; shop.css `.pack-car` is `center / contain no-repeat`, and carstill.js show() gives `contain` to any element whose CSS gives the background no size; drawSoon takes `first` (drawn at once if laid out, else first in the queue; one queued job an element); packCar queues its car first when made and redraws it when its size changes (a ResizeObserver, a step of 20 px). Check: `scripts/.packcar-check.mjs` (new, in the tiers group): boxOf against a shaken box, drawStill's canvas at 2x the span's own size, every car any pack features (the Silver Comet, the Blower) framed whole by PACK_POSE at the big pack's shape and five others (1.4 to 3 : 1; the turned box reaches 0.90 of the edge, the pose's margin), the contain CSS, boxOf in keyOf and drawStill, first + ResizeObserver; it fails on main (no boxOf). Checked: node --check, eslint, `.packcar`, `.packs`, `.shop`, `.carsources`, `.showroom`, `.extras-hit`, `.modelviews` checks, vite build. SEEN in headless Chromium (swiftshader), the big pack held still (animations off) at 1280x800, 1920x1080, 2560x1440, 1366x1024, 2732x2048 and 390x844, each at DPR 1 and 2, `?pack=comet-special` and `?pack=car-hunt-5`: before, at 390x844@2 the Comet (and the Blower) was its nose only, cut at the pack's right and bottom (whole at @1); at the larger sizes main drew no car during the pack stage at all; after, the whole car, centred, at every size and DPR, sharp at 2x. Timing (a trace): the pack's car is now on the first frame after the reveal opens (before the cards). The SAME cut-off was on every small pack too: the Car Hunt event card's pack (`?event=car-hunt`) at 1280x800, 1920x1080, 1366x1024 and 2732x2048 @2 showed only the Blower's nose before (@1 whole) and the whole car after; the Shop's tiles (their packs are turned, so the old box on screen gave the still the wrong shape: 246x143 for a 236x120 span at 2x) are whole after at every size. NOT verified: a real large screen or tablet (the owner's), a real GPU's timing (swiftshader frames take ~0.8 s here, so the cut-off as the owner saw it, a still drawn mid-shake on a real GPU, was reasoned from the code, not reproduced at 1x); a window resized during the shake.

- [x] 217. SHOWROOM: A TAP DURING A PAN RESTARTED IT (the owner, 13-Oct, on a tablet: "On the showroom, tapping left or right while the screen pans resets the animation and becomes really jarring"). Branch `claude/showroom-pan`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 217 is taken). CAUSE: the row's ‹ › arrows (and the arrow keys sideways) used the browser's smooth scroll (`row.scrollBy` / `scrollIntoView`, behavior smooth): each tap began a NEW smooth scroll from where the row was, from standing, with the row's proximity snap pulling in between; the row stood still for frames, started again, and taps were lost. FIX: the pan is ours (src/delivery/render/showroompan.js, pure; driven per frame by render/showroom.js): one tap is the same ease-in-out slide as before (smoothstep, 380 ms, onto a column's start: the same end as before for one tap); a tap during a pan moves the TARGET on by a step from the target (not from where the row is) and the slide carries on from where it is at the speed it has (a cubic Hermite, the speed held so it never passes the target and comes back); a tap the other way turns it round smoothly; the snap is off while it moves (class `panning`) and it ends on a column start so nothing is pulled after; a finger, the mouse or the wheel on the row takes over. The arrow keys (sideways rows) go through the same pan; the phone's column and the phone's single-car slide are unchanged. CHECK: scripts/.showroom-check.mjs `rowPan`: on a fake 60 fps clock, 2 / 4 / 8 taps 70 ms apart never move back, never stand still before the end, never return to the start offset, and end exactly N column steps on; one tap is eased in and out; a reversal turns round with no jumping frame; the ends are kept; the old restart-from-standing model stalls (so the check sees it). SEEN (headless Chromium 1194 through the DevTools protocol, 1280x800, sampling `scrollLeft` every frame): 4 taps 120 ms apart, before: ended at 1786 of about 4080 (taps lost), 2 frames standing still mid-pan; after: 4076, 0 standing, 0 px backwards. 6 taps 70 ms apart, before 1531 (2 standing), after 6112 (0). One tap: 1022 before and after. Max backwards jump per frame was 0 px before and after in headless Chromium (it did not show the snap BACK the owner saw; that is likely the tablet browser's snap/smooth scroll). 8 quick ArrowRight presses: a smooth pan, 0 back, 0 standing. NOT verified: a real tablet (touch on the Xiaomi Pad, its 144 Hz), Safari/iPadOS, how the pan looks to the eye (no stills of motion read), the phone's vertical single-car slide (a CSS animation per car, not this pan; it still restarts per swipe). Checks run: .showroom-check, .touch-check, .nav-check, .extras-hit-check, eslint on the changed files, vite build.

- [x] 234. A CAR MODEL EDITOR (the owner, 13-Oct: "I would like a model editor, I inspect each car, look at what is drawn and manipulate the parts. Level editor but for car JSONs"). Branch `claude/model-editor`, NOT merged, no version bump (placeholder number: renumber at merge if 230 is taken). The models stay CODE; an edit is a PATCH over what the code builds, so the code's hooks (wheels, lamps, livery, animate, suspension) keep working. PAGE `delivery/modeleditor.html` (src/delivery/modeleditor.js, modeleditor.css over the level editor's editor.css): pick any roster car or car idea; a 3D view (orbit / pan / zoom by mouse or one / two fingers, Front / Rear / Left / Right / Top / 3/4, a grid and a soft footprint shadow, Good / Evil, Wire, Bounds, the cars.js Hitbox, the Wheels' centres); the PARTS list (every mesh and group: its name, the code's hook (userData.body, wheels...) or a guessed role: tyre, hub, glass, headlamp, tail lamp, indicator, bumper, mirror, body, part; its kind and triangles; search; eye, solo, lock: this view only); a tap or click in the view selects (raycast; the mouse over a part shows its box), the selected part has a box and a GIZMO (Move G / Rotate R / Scale S, Snap); the PART tab: name, role, position / rotation (degrees) / scale, the sizes of a plain box / cylinder / sphere / cone / torus / plane / circle (when the code did not move it inside its geometry), colour, glow, opacity, "colour every part sharing its material" (one material copy: no extra draw call), "paint for Evil only", shown; Add box / cylinder / sphere / cone, Duplicate (Ctrl+D), Mirror (M: a copy across the car that follows its part), Delete, Reset part; Undo / Redo (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, a stack of patches); the JSON tab (the live patch, typed edits applied when valid); Download / Load (or drop) a patch; autosave per car in localStorage; a STATS strip against CAR-AGENT-PROMPT.md's budget (shapes, triangles, draw calls once merged, edits, stale edits). The page draws with the game's own renderer (render/scene.js on its `<canvas id="game">`: one WebGL context, pixels capped at 2x / 1.5x on touch); render/modeleditorshim.js stands in for the game page's elements that game.js and others grab as they load. THE PATCH (render/modelpatch.js, pure: extractParts, applyPatch, withSet / withDelete / withAdd / withDuplicate / withMirror): `{ car, version, base, ops: [{id, sig, set}|{id, sig, delete}|{add: {id, kind, params, ...}|{id, from, mirror}}], evil: [{id, sig, set}] }`; ids are child-index paths resolved on the model AS BUILT before any change, a sig (geometry type, vertex count, size; not the place) re-finds a part the code moved; an id that finds nothing is reported as unmatched and skipped (never thrown); a different `base` warns that the code changed. Colours clone the material (never one a traffic copy shares); the paint is re-applied after the model's own livery (userData.livery wrapped). THE GAME (render/modelpatchapply.js): `src/delivery/modelpatches/<car id>.json`, loaded lazily (import.meta.glob dynamic imports: none today, the game bundle grew 0.3 kB), applied ONCE per model build where every car model is made: render/showcar.js makeShowCar (garage, inspector, showroom, stills, menu), render/items.js playerModel (the car driven, cached per car), render/ideaslot.js; `?modelpatch=local` uses the editor's patches from this browser, `?modelpatch=off` none. LINKS: Options > Guide "Model editor" next to "Level editor" (both 44 px tall on a touch screen), the level editor's header, the garage inspector's "Edit model" (opens the editor on that car and side in a new tab). TOOLING: `scripts/model-patch.mjs <car> --dump | --check [file] | --apply [file]` and `--all --check`; `scripts/.modelpatch-check.mjs` (in the render group). The replays' rules fingerprint is unchanged (modeleditor.js added to vite.config.js NOT_RULES; no render file is in logicFiles). DOCS: URL-PARAMS.md (modeleditor.html, `modelpatch`), CAR-AGENT-PROMPT.md, the how-to at the top of modeleditor.js.

Z-FIGHTING FROM THE GEOMETRY (the owner, 13-Oct: "Is there a procedural way you can detect all z fighting instances in all the models"): `scripts/.zfight-check.mjs` builds every car headless, takes every visible triangle into world space, finds coplanar overlapping pairs (planes within 4 mm) and keeps only those a viewer's ray meets together from outside (honouring material sides, nothing in front, never from underneath). Writes a report (`--report=<md>`); exit code 1 while any are found, so it is NOT in a test group yet. First (corrected) run: 270 pairs in 66 cars: 82 will flicker (under 0.5 mm apart), 110 might (0.5-4 mm), 78 are doubled same-colour surfaces (no flicker). The detector is `render/zfight.js`, also used by the model editor's Z-fight tab (scan, click a row to see both surfaces, Lift A/B to stand 5 mm in front, Delete A/B). THE OWNER DECIDES which surface wins for each; then fix, then add the script to the render group.

- [x] 257. TRUE SPEED (the owner, 14-Oct: "Lets implement true speed" = real lane lengths on bends; then "Lane changes should scrub some speed as well... part of the agility stat? It still wouldn't be very significant"). Branch `claude/charming-maxwell-pxoail`, NOT merged, no version bump. `CONFIG.trueSpeed` { on, least 0.4, most 2.5, laneScrub 1.2 }; `Track.pathScale(s, lat)` = 1 / (1 - bend x lat), so progress along the road is the distance driven over the length of the car's own line (inside of a bend shorter, outside longer). Applied to the player (`this.s += speed*dt*pathScale`) and to ordinary traffic / racers (traffic.js main advance). Lane changes scrub `laneScrub x across^2 / agility` m/s^2 (across = sideways speed over the car's full sideways speed), never below minSpeed: under 1 m/s for a lane change at 40 m/s, and a nimble car loses less. NOT changed: the screensaver dolly, special traffic states (flyovers, ramps, pursuit), the lap/finish maths (they use s, so a tight inside line is simply faster). REPLAYS: the rules fingerprint changes (player.js / track.js / config.js / traffic.js are inside it), old replays and ghosts are refused (accepted by the owner). CLEAN-RUN TIMES (scripts/level-clocks.mjs --all, flag on vs off; the run keeps the centre of its lane): 34 of 44 timed levels within 0.5%; circuits slower by 3.5-10.6% (grand-prix 222.8 -> 230.6, spa 198.5 -> 207.4, marina-bay 216.9 -> 229.1, montreal 190.1 -> 201.8, stelvio 179.8 -> 191.2, mystery-meadows 98.0 -> 108.4): the clean run drives the lane centre, which on a mostly outside-heavy line is longer. The written level clocks / par / medal times are NOT re-written yet (a decision for the owner). CHECKED: `scripts/.truespeed-check.mjs` (new), level-clocks both ways. NOT verified: driving it in a browser (how it feels), rivals' races, ghost racing across lanes, the tiers group after the change.

NOT verified: the wider 30-car audit, other views, Evil liveries, night, the game's own camera, dents in a real level, a phone. WATCH: a damaged player car is 1.5-6x the triangles (commuter 1,764 -> 8,288, junker 2,146 -> 13,158, darkvan 10,689, lowrider 11,762) because low-poly panels are subdivided; the owner should decide if that is acceptable on the Mi Pad.

Branch `claude/damage-audit` (from 9cded11), NOT merged, no version bump. Render-side only (`src/delivery/render/dents.js`, `scripts/.dents-check.mjs`; config and the rules files are untouched). The four stages stay; what changed: (1) parts that are not paint now follow the dents too: small ones (lamps, grilles, mirrors, under 0.35 m) are moved whole by the dents at their centre, larger ones (bumpers, stripes, pinstripes, a roof or cab in a paint of its own, a tarp, a canopy) are bent like the panels. Glass (found by its colour), the wheels and what turns with them (found by render/carsuspension.js), see-through and instanced meshes, and the lamp effects are left alone. (2) The dents are held back: near the glass the paint does not move, so it never goes behind it; nothing gets nearer than 0.6 of its distance to a wheel or the glass; a shove toward the middle stops 0.45 of the way there. The table is now gentle enough that the dents never fold the car through itself (checked numerically: the dent field's Jacobian stays positive). (3) Panels are cut finer once per model, only where the dents bend them (an edge is halved where the dents at its middle differ from the mean of its ends by more than 2 cm, or 5 mm where paint and another part lie together). The cut is kept, and is made on a timer when the car is first seen, not on the first knock. (4) A dent's reach and depth are capped at a big car's size in metres (1.0 / 1.6 / 2.4 m), so a bus is dented at its corners, not along half its length. (5) Stages 1 and 2 were re-authored (a front corner, the tail and a door, then a creased bonnet and a caved door), and the whole table rescaled. Bug fixed on the way: the crumpled copies were computed in the frame the car stood in when first measured, so a car that had driven on was dented in the wrong place. Triangles (the player's car only, at any stage; clean is unchanged): commuter 1764 to 8288, sportscompact 1000 to 10582, junker 2146 to 13158, darkvan 1236 to 10689, city bus 1020 to 2542, lowrider 1984 to 11762, F1 676 to 1524, tuk-tuk 1112 to 3922. Measuring and cutting takes 50 to 300 ms in Node, once per model.
  - CHECKED: `.dents-check` (rewritten: glass and wheels untouched, a low-poly panel cut finer, corners together, a lamp moved whole, a stripe bent and not swallowed, nothing past the middle or into a wheel, clean again, the same dents after the car has moved and turned); eslint on both files; `--group render --no-lock` (10 of 10 pass); vite build. LOOKED at (headless Chromium/swiftshader contact sheets, studio light, clean and stages 1 to 4, front and rear three-quarters) on the commuter, sportscompact, junker, darkvan, city bus, lowrider, F1 and tuk-tuk, compared with the first version and the old procedural damage.
  - NOT verified: the other cars (the wider 30-car audit was not done); side, top and Evil views (the lowrider's top and front were seen once); the game's own box car (`makeCarMesh`, used by cars with no model) was only checked headless; in a run (the game camera, night, the ghost); the 50 to 300 ms of measuring on a phone; the measuring before the first knock is on a timer, not proven to land before it. Remaining faults seen: the city bus barely shows its dents (most of it is glass, held still); the darkvan's stripes still break where they wrap the crushed rear corner; small black flecks where a badge pokes through the crushed nose (commuter); the junker's cab roof does not dent (held by the windows).

One choice for every car: Stock + Silver, Black, Gold, White, Red, Blue, Green, Orange recolours the HUBS (rims, hub caps, spokes), tyres stay black. `render/wheelcosmetic.js` (render-only: nothing is written on a car's entry; no rules module imports it, so it is not in the replay fingerprint, checked against vite.config.js `logicFiles`). Hubs are found geometrically when a model is built (`dressWheels`, called from render/showcar.js `makeShowCar`: garage, showroom, menu card, inspector, stills, pack banners, unlock cinematic; and render/items.js `playerModel`: the car driven): tyres = discs on their side touching the floor (as render/carsuspension.js), hub = any other mesh centred on a tyre's axle, within the tread's size and about the tyre's width, not in the tyre's own colour. Materials are cloned per model (never the shared cached ones); Stock gives the original back; a ghost's stand-in keeps the new paint under it (solidMat); a blackout silhouette is left alone; Legendary cars keep their gold hubs; dents.js got one guard so a blue hub clone is never taken for glass. - [x] 269. WHEEL DESIGNS, extending 268 (the owner, 14-Oct: "designs that look plausible spinning first, like whitewalls, and red with white hubs"; branch `claude/wheel-designs`, NOT merged, version not bumped). A design = tyre treatment + hub treatment, all rings / discs / bands (rotationally symmetric: reads the same still or turning; no spokes). Six presets: Stock; Whitewall (white ring, 0.64 to 0.88 of the tyre radius, on the outer sidewall); Redline (thin red ring, 0.78 to 0.84); Red tyres, white hubs (rubber recoloured red, hubs white); White hubs on black; Gold hubs, whitewall. Per wheel: a ring is one RingGeometry (16 segments = 32 triangles; one shared geometry per ring shape, one shared material per colour, both flagged shared), 5 mm off the tyre's outer face, a child of the tyre mesh so it rides with the planted wheel; a centre wheel (trike front) gets one each side (64). Colour-only designs add no triangles. Rubber recolour (tyre, tread blocks, sidewall rings) uses per-model clones, never the shared cached materials. Interaction with 268: the hub colour row still works and wins: a hub colour other than Stock overrides the design's hubs (tyres keep the design); hub colour Stock keeps the design's own hubs (or the car's). Hub clones also get a little emissive (30%) so white reads white in shade (this lightly brightens the 268 colours too). Only tyres that have a hub of their own get treated; a car with no hub found stays stock (Tank; no-tyre cars; Legendary stay stock by design). Kept in localStorage `delivery.wheeldesign` (try/catch, ignored and unsaved under Progress.noSave); `?wheeldesign=<id>` for stills. UI: Options sheet, Wheels section now has a "Design" chip row (little round picture of tyre/ring/hub each) above the "Hub colour" row, 44 px on touch (menu.js, delivery/index.html, menu2.css). Guards added: render/carsuspension.js and render/dents.js skip meshes flagged `userData.wheelRing`. Hit rate over the 204 roster cars built (garage, filler, Bespoke, event, earned, ideas, Supers): designs reach 184 cars and 763 of 769 tyres (the 6 left: Tank, 2 of the Dragster's wheels, etc., as 268); no tyres: earned jetboat, earned UFO, Monowheel, Snocat, Hovercraft; Legendary 14 stock. Verified: `scripts/.wheelcosmetic-check.mjs` extended (all ok: per design a ring per hubbed tyre, 32 tris, one shared geometry, clones, nothing else changed, stock materials unmutated, colour wins over design hub, Stock restores and removes rings, no ring pile-up, built-under-design, ghost, blackout, suspension finder unchanged, dents unchanged and ring geometry untouched, z-fight finder with rings on, new key reload / garbage / blocked storage / noSave); node --check and eslint; .bundle-check (only the existing EVERGREEN_CARS warning); .dents-check; .zfight-check (summary unchanged: it builds without dressWheels, so the ring case is covered by the check above); `delivery-tests --group render --no-lock` (10 ok); vite build. Stills read in a headless browser (/tmp/wheel-designs-agent): commuter hatch, pickup, minivan, Super Sport, a dented minivan (25% health) in 4 designs each, Redline and White-hubs-on-black on a wagon, Options on desktop and phone width (44 px chips). - [x] 278. COSMETIC OWNERSHIP, "Absolutely everything" includes all cosmetics (the owner, 14-Oct; branch `claude/unlock-cosmetics`, from `claude/wheel-sets`, NOT merged, version not bumped). Plumbing only: every cosmetic is still free, so nothing the player sees is locked yet. OWNER DECISION (14-Oct): "cosmetics should not break the hash. If the cosmetic is unavailable then oh well", so cosmetics are NOT part of the replay fingerprint and `progress.js` is byte-identical to origin/main. (1) REGISTRY `src/delivery/render/cosmetics.js` (in render/ because every top-level src/delivery file not in NOT_RULES is walked into the fingerprint, as a first try at src/delivery/cosmetics.js showed): `COSMETICS` of `{ slot, id, name, free }`, `registerCosmetics([...])` (returns an undo, for a check's fixture), `cosmeticKeys()` ('wheels:whitewall-silver' style, free ones too), `isFreeCosmetic`. `render/wheelcosmetic.js` registers its 12 `WHEEL_SETS` (all free). To add a cosmetic: call `registerCosmetics` from its slot's module as it loads (a new slot's module is also imported by unlockall.js so 'abs' sees it). (2) OWNERSHIP: the same module keeps a list of owned 'slot:id' keys in its OWN localStorage key `delivery.cosmetics` (JSON array, cleaned on read: text keys of the right shape, no repeats, at most 300; every access in try/catch; on a Progress.noSave test visit a grant lasts the visit and writes nothing; the menu's Reset progress clears it). `ownsCosmetic(slot, id)` = free or listed; `grantCosmetics(keys)`; `ownedCosmetics()`; `clearCosmetics()`. Not in the cookie and not in progress.js's save at all. KNOWN LIMIT: because the save code (exportCode / tidy) is in progress.js, ownership is not carried by a save code; it stays in the browser. (3) `cosmeticsui.js` `owned(slot, id)` asks `ownsCosmetic`; a refused chip shows locked (existing CSS) and cannot be equipped; an equipped SAVED wheel set the player does not own wears Stock (wheelcosmetic.js `savedPairOwned`; the saved ids are kept, so owning it brings it back); an id that does not exist (design, colour or set) already resolves to Stock and setters ignore it, never an error. A replay or ghost never refers to wheels (they are a local render choice), so none can be refused over one. (4) `unlockEverything('abs')` also grants every registry key; 'all' is unchanged; unlockall.js header, the confirm text in menu.js and the note in delivery/index.html say cosmetics are included. (5) `scripts/.cosmetics-own-check.mjs` with a fixture (a non-free plate and wheel set, never shipped): locked by default, still locked after 'all', unlocked after 'abs', kept through a reload from its localStorage key, the cookie AND the whole progress save byte-identical with the list granted and cleared, cleaner, broken/blocked storage, noSave, equipped-but-not-owned shows Stock, unknown ids show Stock, and the REPLAY FINGERPRINT: it extracts origin/main with git archive and computes vite.config.js `logicFiles` hash on both: origin/main 4e6f6f1661 = this branch 4e6f6f1661 (same file list), and progress.js is byte-identical. Verified: .cosmetics-own-check, .save-check, .wheelcosmetic-check, .schema-check, .bundle-check (only the existing EVERGREEN_CARS warning), `delivery-tests --group render --no-lock`, vite build, eslint on the changed files. NOT verified: in a real browser (no still taken; the Options button, the tooltip and the confirm text were not looked at); a non-free item in the real Cosmetics screen (only the logic, in the fixture); the cookie size in a real browser against the 4096 cap (unchanged by construction: COOKIE_KEYS untouched and progress.js identical); ownership across devices (needs the save code, a known limit).
- [x] 285. ECU UPGRADES COST BANK (the owner, 14-Oct: "ECU levels should cost bank to upgrade. Collecting the blueprints only unblocks the upgrade"; branch `claude/ecu-upgrade-cost`, from main v1.1.129 + wheel sets, NOT merged, version not bumped). BUILT, barebones: a car now has a REACHED ECU (`Ecu.reached`: its blueprints, X per step, capped by the ladder, exactly as before) and a PURCHASED one (`Ecu.purchased`, saved in `Progress.data.ecuBought = { [carId]: ECU }`, only cars above their free first ECU have an entry); it PLAYS at the purchased one (`Ecu.level`). Rule chosen for blueprint cars (a QUESTION for the owner): ECU1 stays FREE (a key car: its key; a blueprint car: its first X blueprints unlock it, as ever, it is "owned" by them), every ECU above is a purchase from the bank (`Progress.data.money`, never Crazey Cash), one step at a time, in order, only up to the reached ECU and only with the key (`Ecu.upgrade(car)` says why not: none / maxed / nokey / locked / blueprints (needs N more) / short (needs $N more) / ready; `Ecu.buy(car)`). PRICES: `CONFIG.ecuCost` in config.js (PLACEHOLDERS for the balancing pass): step to ECU n = ref(stars) x 0.2 x 1.5^(n-2), rounded to $5, ref = a typical car lot price of the stars (gold 50/170/310/450/700, blue 290/450/730/1000/1270, no stars 1500), `perCar` overrides; maxing costs 0.16 to 1.8 x the car's own lot price (a 2-ECU car $35, a gold 5 $1,140 with a last step of $475, a blue 5 $2,060, an event/Bespoke car $2,440); the table: `node scripts/.ecu-cost-check.mjs --table`. UI: the showroom's full view (render/ecuupgrade.js + ecuupgrade.css) shows, for a car whose next ECU is in sight, an "Upgrade to ECU3: $1,200" button (green when it can be bought; dimmed with "Need $N more in the bank" or "Needs N more blueprints to unlock ECU3" when not; 44 px, keyboard, `aria-describedby` the reason) and the figures the step changes (ecus.json, unchanged); buying redraws the showroom at once (bank, pips, live car, figures), no reload. The pips show bought (filled) and unlocked-not-bought (outlined). MIGRATION: a save with no `ecuBought` reads as null and `Ecu.migrate()` (run when ecu.js loads, before blueprints are added, and before a save is written) fills it from the blueprint levels (every car keeps the ECU it has: no refund, no charge; a car in `maxed` needs no entry), once, idempotent; `maxed` means what it meant. "Unlock everything" (both modes): every car it deals is bought up to its max, a blueprint car blueprints had unlocked up to what they reached; mode abs otherwise unchanged. Address switches: `&bp=` (showroom) now only gives blueprints (it unlocks an upgrade); NEW `&ecu=midnight:1,sport:2` sets purchased levels for the visit, `&money=` the bank; `&owns=` still maxes the car (as before). COOKIE: `ecuBought` follows the blueprints (in the cookie, dropped with them and `maxed` if the cookie is too long, so the 4096 cap holds): a fresh save +27 bytes, every one of the 36 lot cars fully bought +714 bytes (measured by `.ecu-cost-check`, which reads document.cookie in the headless page); local storage has the whole save. Files in the replay logic hash that changed (checked with vite.config.js logicFiles()): `config.js`, `ecu.js`, `progress.js`, `showroomData.js` (ecus.json NOT touched; main.js, unlockall.js, packs.js are outside the hash). Free tries (`ownEcu` events, events.js / ecu.js `drive`) still play at the REACHED ECU (a key car without its key cannot be bought up, and the Concept Strike key needs the max ECU). CHECKED: `node --check` and eslint on changed files; `.ecu-check` (updated: it plays cars at the ECU their blueprints reached with a helper), `.save-check` (extended: purchase saved, old save migrated), `.ecu-cost-check` (NEW: 70+ checks: price table monotonic, buying deducts exactly the price and never below zero, no buy above reached or without a key, blueprints alone do not raise the playing ECU, migration of old saves/codes/pre-ECU saves idempotent, Unlock everything both modes, cookie size), `.unlockall-check`, `.bundle-check`, `.schema-check`, `.packs-check`, `.events-check`, `.shop-check`, `.carsources(-dom)-check`, `.showroom-check`, `vite build`; render group of `delivery-tests` (see the commit message for the result). `.strike-levels-check` fails ("the day's car maxed takes about 50 s (65.0 s)") on main too: not caused by this. NOT verified: nobody has played it by hand; the car lot's (garage) own screen shows the purchased ECU in its stats line but has no Upgrade button (the showroom is where cars are picked); the pack reveal's "ECU UP" glow and the ECU-up cinematic (ecucine.js) still fire when blueprints REACH a new ECU, which now only unlocks the upgrade; the prices are untested for feel; replays recorded before are refused by the changed hash (accepted before).
